## E0001 TRAINER_BRENDAN_ROUTE_103_MUDKIP class=rival
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Pikachu uses Fake Out only on an interruptible threat or Electroweb when the speed drop enables its partner; the starter attacks or sets up only behind a genuinely safe turn. On turn two, exploit the created speed/position advantage rather than repeat Fake Out. Alternate deployment: Pikachu and Shroomish trade immediate starter pressure for disruption and recovery. Reserves & disruption: Taillow is an Orb-activation finisher, not a guaranteed turn-one Guts attacker. Preserve an attack line when sleep is blocked; respect the actual unchosen regional starter. Counterplay & audit: Grass/powder immunity, Electric immunity, priority denial, double targeting, and denying free setup. Disposition: Keep the printed Hoenn seeds; regional generated starter sets require the explicit expansion register, not a false claim that six printed aliases cover all generations.
crack: Grass/powder immunity, Electric immunity, priority denial, double targeting, and denying free setup. Disposition: Keep the printed Hoenn seeds; regional generated starter sets require the explicit expansion register, not a false claim that six printed aliases cover all generations.

## E0002 TRAINER_CALVIN_1 class=regular
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Poochyena uses Taunt or Helping Hand according to whether denying setup or securing damage matters more; Zigzagoon drums only when its post-cost HP and legal Gluttony/Figy recovery survive the opposing pair. On the next turn, cash in with Extreme Speed rather than continue setting up. Alternate deployment: Hoothoot Tailwind plus Starly gives an immediate attacking mode without Belly Drum. Reserves & disruption: If Drum is unsafe, attack with Zigzagoon or move to the bird pair. Figy is compatible with Adamant; Unnerve, item suppression or removal can deny recovery. Rattled is an opponent-dependent bonus, not an activation plan. Counterplay & audit: Priority immunity/denial, Fake Out on the support, physical mitigation, berry denial or focused damage before Drum resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Figy replaces Sitrus to make Gluttony meaningful. Benchmark the first forced route fight against multiple starter pairs, not just a compliant setup fixture.
crack: Priority immunity/denial, Fake Out on the support, physical mitigation, berry denial or focused damage before Drum resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Figy replaces Sitrus to make Gluttony meaningful. Benchmark the first forced route fight against multiple starter pairs, not just a compliant setup fixture.

## E0003 TRAINER_RICK class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Scarf Spinarak chooses String Shot when the two-stage Speed drops let Dwebble or Karrablast act usefully before the player; Electroweb offers damaging one-stage control when that is enough. Turn two switches Spinarak when its locked support action no longer produces value. Alternate deployment: Fast control beside Karrablast creates stronger immediate offensive conversion. Reserves & disruption: Dewpider supplies the special Water lane when physical attacks are contained. Do not let a Choice-locked utility move become repeated empty turns. Re-evaluate both targets, turn order, protection, stat-loss immunity, Contrary and retaliatory abilities. Counterplay & audit: Ground immunity blocks Electroweb damage but does not inherently block String Shot. Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, White Herb, speed reversal and punishing switches offer counterplay. Disposition: RETAIN; String Shot replaces Toxic Thread for direct paired speed control, while the Scarf control-and-switch identity remains.
crack: Ground immunity blocks Electroweb damage but does not inherently block String Shot. Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, White Herb, speed reversal and punishing switches offer counterplay. Disposition: RETAIN; String Shot replaces Toxic Thread for direct paired speed control, while the Scarf control-and-switch identity remains.

## E0004 TRAINER_ALLEN class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Pikachu can buy Paras a Spore or use its own damage while Paras selects Wide Guard against real spread pressure. On turn two, attack or reposition; do not automatically shield both supports. Alternate deployment: Pikachu and Bunnelby create a faster damage-focused opening; Mienfoo can supply Fake Out while Paras acts or pressure Rock/Steel targets that resist Bunnelby's Normal attacks. Reserves & disruption: Mienfoo's Eviolite and Regenerator support repeatable U-turn positioning with Bunnelby. Choose a legal useful reserve, account for entry hazards and the incoming attack, and credit Regenerator only when a switch actually occurs. Drain Punch supplies an attacking recovery line; Knock Off disrupts equipment. Paras is not protected from single-target double targeting by Wide Guard. Counterplay & audit: Powder immunity, spread-versus-targeted mix-ups, Ghost immunity to Fake Out/Fighting attacks, Psychic/Fairy pressure on Mienfoo, trapping and punishing the incoming reserve. Disposition: RETAIN; Mienfoo replaces the unsupported Truant/Choice Band Slakoth role. Preserve Bunnelby's Frustration friendship zero. Do not force switches or Fake Out when an immediate attack is better.
crack: Powder immunity, spread-versus-targeted mix-ups, Ghost immunity to Fake Out/Fighting attacks, Psychic/Fairy pressure on Mienfoo, trapping and punishing the incoming reserve. Disposition: RETAIN; Mienfoo replaces the unsupported Truant/Choice Band Slakoth role. Preserve Bunnelby's Frustration friendship zero. Do not force switches or Fake Out when an immediate attack is better.

## E0005 TRAINER_TIANA class=casual
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Skitty chooses Fake Out when interruption is necessary, or timely Fake Tears on the same target Swirlix can exploit with special damage; Helping Hand remains the damage-support alternative when defense drops are blocked or less useful. Do not spend a turn lowering Special Defense after the relevant partner attack has already happened. Alternate deployment: Buneary threatens Klutz-assisted Flame Orb Switcheroo while Swirlix attacks. Reserves & disruption: Eevee can receive a Wish on a planned switch or keep itself alive for another attack. An unconsumed Sitrus does not grant Unburden, and switching removes the earned speed bonus. Fake Tears must respect protection, reflection, stat-loss immunity, Contrary, White Herb and retaliatory abilities; it does not help Eevee's physical attacks. Counterplay & audit: Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, status immunity, item protection where supported, and burst damage before Wish resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Fake Tears replaces Sing to give the whimsical support core a reliable special-damage partnership. No automatic credit for Unburden or guaranteed Orb disruption.
crack: Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, status immunity, item protection where supported, and burst damage before Wish resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Fake Tears replaces Sing to give the whimsical support core a reliable special-damage partnership. No automatic credit for Unburden or guaranteed Orb disruption.

## E0006 TRAINER_BILLY class=regular
strategy: RAIN
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Lotad chooses Rain Dance only when Ducklett's next attack and its own rain turns repay the support action; Ducklett can use Icy Wind or a safe attack while weather is established. Turn two exploits rain instead of resetting it unnecessarily. Alternate deployment: Lotad and Tandemaus combine weather pressure with Encore/Population Bomb rather than another Water attacker. Reserves & disruption: Ditto is a reactive reserve, not an arbitrary turn-one transformation. Ducklett Surf still hits Lotad despite its resistance; use a safe attack when ally damage loses the exchange. Counterplay & audit: Weather replacement, Wide Guard, Grass/Electric pressure, and denying a favorable Ditto target. Disposition: Preserve the rain lesson; attach an ally-damage decision to Surf rather than claiming rain partners are immune.
crack: Weather replacement, Wide Guard, Grass/Electric pressure, and denying a favorable Ditto target. Disposition: Preserve the rain lesson; attach an ally-damage decision to Surf rather than claiming rain partners are immune.

## E0007 TRAINER_DARIAN class=regular
strategy: SETUP,PRESSURE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Chinchou's Discharge is a safe ally line beside Ground-type Barboach while Barboach either attacks or uses Dragon Dance behind adequate survival. Turn two evaluates the new speed order before spending another setup turn. Alternate deployment: Tentacool resists neither Ground nor Electric but takes Grass neutrally and threatens Grass attackers with Sludge Bomb. Acid Spray supports an actual special follow-up from Chinchou or Clamperl; value same-turn support only when it lands before the partner attack. Clamperl retains the special Shell Smash mode. Reserves & disruption: Eviolite gives Tentacool working bulk; Liquid Ooze punishes eligible draining attacks, not all Grass damage. Muddy Water attacks both opponents without allied damage. Discharge damages Tentacool and Clamperl: re-evaluate every switch and choose a safer action when warranted. Counterplay & audit: Ground/Electric pressure on Tentacool, Grass pressure on the other three, Taunt, immediate damage and revenge priority. Disposition: Approved Corphish replacement removes the universal Grass weakness while preserving the Chinchou/Barboach engine and marine theme. Shared support forecasts honor order and stat-drop blockers; no forced Acid Spray or Discharge script.
crack: Ground/Electric pressure on Tentacool, Grass pressure on the other three, Taunt, immediate damage and revenge priority. Disposition: Approved Corphish replacement removes the universal Grass weakness while preserving the Chinchou/Barboach engine and marine theme. Shared support forecasts honor order and stat-drop blockers; no forced Acid Spray or Discharge script.

mega_slots: NONE

plan: First two useful turns: Snubbull's Intimidate softens eligible attackers while Milcery selects Helping Hand or an active Fairy attack. Reserves & disruption: Slakoth is a one-hit reserve with an exit; Aroma Veil protects only its enumerated effects and is not a blanket anti-disruption shield. Counterplay & audit: Special offense, Defiant/Competitive, Taunt outside Aroma Veil's protected set, and targeting Milcery. Disposition: Keep the species identity; reject any AI trace that values pure durability above protecting an actual threat.

## E0009 TRAINER_LYLE class=casual
strategy: PRESSURE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Nymble uses First Impression only on its eligible entry turn and otherwise attacks or pivots; Sewaddle supplies Bug/Grass damage or Struggle Bug mitigation. The fourth slot is a native regional substitution: all six gender/Hoenn aliases and the 24 non-Hoenn middle-stage alternatives must remain coherent. Hoenn keeps its printed bespoke sets; Marshtomp Earthquake is safe beside Taillow but not Electrike/Shroomish. Prinplup's replacement uses Hydro Pump instead of unsupported Scald; Drizzile uses Mud Shot instead of unsupported Ice Beam, preserving Sniper/Focus Energy and adding Electric coverage/speed control. No automatic sun or ally Electric activation is assumed. Counterplay: physical attacks bypass Eerie Impulse, powder/status immunity, priority, special-drop prevention and attacking the exposed partner during Protect. Design: continuity earns retaining the familiar species, with differentiated support and individual Medium level seeds; full native branch/difficulty acceptance remains pending.
crack: Physical pressure, powder immunity, stat-drop prevention, priority and punishing a passive Orb turn; each regional starter changes the matchup.

## E0321 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_1 class=grunt
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room one of the raid, the slag door. First two useful turns: Glimmora attacks with Power Gem or Sludge Wave; if the player answers it with a physical hit, Toxic Debris lays Toxic Spikes for free and every later Magma room profits, so Mortal Spin is only for clearing the player's own hazards. Swalot Yawns the target it cannot remove more profitably and Encores a committed setup or protect. Alternate deployment: Camerupt is the faction mount and the room's only real damage spike; Mightyena is the faction dog with Intimidate and a Band. Reserves & disruption: Toxic Spikes are the hideout's shared opening investment; a grounded Poison switch-in absorbs them. Counterplay & audit: fly or levitate past the spikes, Ground and Water pressure, Taunt the Yawn, and Safety Goggles or Misty ground are not answers here. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it now is: the old block was Swalot/Camerupt/Alolan Persian/Houndoom, two of which belong to other rooms and one of which (Persian) is a sleek casino cat with no business in a volcano. Glimmora is the memorable upgrade - a rare Rock/Poison crystal-flower whose Toxic Debris turns the player's first physical answer into a hideout-wide hazard, which is exactly the "door crew softens you up for the rest of the base" story. Mightyena and Camerupt are the two deliberate Magma uniform species, used on exactly two grunt rooms each and nowhere else in the hideout.
crack: Airborne or Poison-typed leads ignore the spikes; Ground and Water pressure removes Glimmora and Camerupt; Taunt shuts Yawn and Encore; do not feed Toxic Debris with the obvious physical answer on turn one.

Alternate deployment: Golem's Sturdy plus Weakness Policy means a resisted-but-super-effective hit hands it a free +2/+2, and Donphan's Sturdy gives the room a second one-turn floor. Reserves & disruption: sand chips every non Rock/Ground/Steel body on the player's side for the whole room and is the setup the deeper sand rooms reuse. It is cooler now because the room is a pure excavation crew: one weather setter, one Sand Rush sweeper and two Sturdy bodies that make the player spend real actions to break a floor before the sweeper connects. It is deliberately the simplest engine in the hideout so that the later rooms read as escalation.

## E0324 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_3 class=grunt
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room four, the relic dig, and the hideout's junior Trick Room - deliberately a smaller rehearsal of the room Maxie himself runs at the bottom. First two useful turns: Claydol sets Trick Room only when it survives the turn and at least one of Bronzong, Runerigus or Garbodor is on the field to spend it; otherwise it attacks with Earth Power. Bronzong under reversed order fires Gyro Ball at its real 33 Speed, which is close to maximum power. Alternate deployment: Runerigus' Wandering Spirit swaps abilities with a contact attacker, which can strand a Guts, Speed Boost or Protean sweeper with a useless ability for the rest of the fight. Reserves & disruption: Garbodor lays Toxic Spikes only if the door crew's set was removed, otherwise it attacks; Aftermath punishes the contact finish. Counterplay & audit: Taunt the setter, run out the five turns behind Protect, or use Fighting and Fire pressure that does not need contact. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Claydol/Garbodor/Umbreon/Camerupt - a second Camerupt in the same hideout and an Umbreon, which is a moonlight Eeveelution and about as un-Magma as a Pokemon gets. It is cooler now because every member is something the crew dug out of the volcano - a clay idol, a temple bell, a cursed coffin lid and the base's own garbage - and because Wandering Spirit gives the room a signature the player will remember: punching Runerigus can cost you your ability for the rest of the battle.
crack: Taunt or KO Claydol before the room turns; Protect through the five turns; Wandering Spirit only triggers on contact, so spread and special attacks are safe answers.

## E0325 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP

First two useful turns: Scrafty's Fake Out buys exactly one turn and is spent on the turn Magcargo actually wants - Simple Shell Smash is +4/+4/+2 in one action, and the White Herb immediately restores the defence drops, so the room's whole plan is to survive to that second turn. Alternate deployment: if Magcargo is not safe, Revavroom takes Shift Gear instead and the room becomes a physical Steel/Poison race. Magcargo going from a joke to a +4 attacker in one turn is the memory the room is built to leave.
crack: Fake Out, Taunt, priority and spread damage all beat a 50 HP setup sweeper; Haze or Clear Smog undoes the Smash; Ground and Water attacks remove Magcargo outright.

## E0327 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_15 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room seven, the ambush on the stairs. First two useful turns: Incineroar opens with Fake Out and its Intimidate has already cut the player's physical lead, then Armarouge fires Armor Cannon - 120 base power that drops its own defences, which is exactly the trade an ambush crew makes. Alternate deployment: Klawf's Anger Shell means the player's own strong hit flips it to +1 Attack, +1 Special Attack and +1 Speed below half HP, so chipping it is worse than killing it. Reserves & disruption: Muk's Poison Touch poisons through contact and Parting Shot from Incineroar resets a bad board. Armarouge and Klawf are both rare, both visually volcanic, and neither appears anywhere else in the campaign.
crack: Kill Klawf outright or leave it alone; Water, Ground and Rock pressure; punish Armarouge on the turn after Armor Cannon; Fake Out or priority denies Incineroar's opening.

## E0328 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_6 class=grunt
strategy: REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room eight, the web gallery, and the hideout's one redirection room. First two useful turns: Ariados chooses between Sticky Web, which slows every later Magma room's opposition for the rest of this battle, and Rage Powder, which buys Infernape or Magmortar a clean turn - it does not do both, and Insomnia means sleep will not shortcut the choice. Infernape uses Fake Out only when the bought turn is actually worth more than immediate damage. Alternate deployment: Magmortar behind redirection is the room's real damage, with Heat Wave into both slots. Reserves & disruption: Nidoking's Sheer Force Earth Power is the answer to Fire-immune and Rock bodies. Counterplay & audit: Safety Goggles, Grass typing and airborne Pokemon all ignore Rage Powder; spread moves ignore redirection entirely. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Ariados/Infernape/Magmortar/Turtonator, which was already coherent - the change is Turtonator out for Nidoking, because the room needed a Ground lane to threaten the Rock and Fire-immune answers that the rest of the hideout has been teaching the player to bring, and Turtonator does more good in the deep sun room. The signature here stays the honest doubles question Rage Powder asks: spend a spread move and waste the redirection, or aim single-target and lose the turn.
crack: Safety Goggles, a Grass-typed or airborne lead, or any spread move makes Rage Powder worthless; Rock and Water pressure removes the Fire pair; Sticky Web is answered by Levitate, Flying types or Defiant.

strategy: NONE

Centiskorch is the standout: a giant burning centipede is exactly what should be feeding a volcano's furnace, and Coil fixing Fire Lash's accuracy is a real, visible combo rather than a stat stick.

## E0331 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_9 class=grunt
strategy: ALLY_COMBO

Stonjourner is also exactly the right image for a volcano base - a henge the crew dug up and stood back on its feet - and this is Camerupt's second and last grunt appearance.

## E0332 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_16 class=grunt

Orthworm, Toedscruel and Pangoro are all first appearances.

## E0335 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_12 class=grunt
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room fifteen, the caldera floor, and the rehearsal for Courtney's sun. First two useful turns: Torkoal's Drought is automatic, so its first action is a full-HP Eruption - 150 base power, sun-boosted, into both of the player's slots - and the room is designed so the player must answer that before turn two. Reserves & disruption: Garchomp is the non-sun lane and its Rough Skin punishes contact. It is cooler now because the room is a two-stage threat that teaches the boss fight: Eruption punishes you for not acting, and the thing that buys Eruption a second turn is a Fire-typed Rage Powder user you cannot answer with Safety Goggles habits alone. Chip Torkoal and the room's damage collapses - that is the lesson the player needs before Maxie.

## E0336 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_13 class=grunt
strategy: SAND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room sixteen, the iron gate outside the admins' chamber, and the hardest grunt fight in the campaign so far. First two useful turns: Tyranitar's Sand Stream overwrites whatever weather the player installed in the last room and its Assault Vest makes the special answer to it fail; it attacks immediately. Probopass' Magnet Pull traps the player's Steel switch-ins - the exact Pokemon a player brings to answer a Rock and Ground hideout - and Sturdy plus sand makes it a four-turn problem. Alternate deployment: Steelix takes Wide Guard against the player's spread answer and Body Press converts its enormous Defense into damage. Reserves & disruption: Garchomp is absent here; this room deliberately has no fast member, so the player who solved earlier rooms with speed has to solve this one with damage. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Rhyperior/Amoonguss/Pangoro/Tauros - a mushroom and a cow standing between the player and Maxie. Trapping the player's Steel pivot with Magnet Pull immediately before the admin gauntlet is the most consequential grunt decision in the hideout.
crack: Do not bring a Steel pivot you need to rotate; Fighting, Water and Ground pressure; Taunt on Steelix; the room has no speed control, so fast special attackers dictate the pace.

## E0337 TRAINER_TABITHA_MAGMA_HIDEOUT class=admin
strategy: SAND,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Tabitha runs the excavation, so his battle is the hideout's sand engine executed properly. First two useful turns: Gigalith's Sand Stream with Smooth Rock lasts eight turns and Excadrill's Sand Rush doubles its Speed for all of them, so the opening is Gigalith attacking while Excadrill outruns almost anything the player owns. Gigalith's own alternative action is Wide Guard when the player's answer to a Rock-heavy admin is a spread move. Alternate deployment: Coalossal's Steam Engine is NOT self-activated here - the previous Mt Chimney fight already used that trick and repeating it would be a weaker second showing. Instead Coalossal is a trap: any Fire or Water attack the player aims at it, including the Water attack that is 4x super effective and the obvious answer to a Rock/Fire body, grants +6 Speed if it survives, and Weakness Policy grants +2/+2 on top. Killing it outright is correct; chipping it is a disaster. Reserves & disruption: Volcanion is the faction's captured volcano spirit and Steam Eruption off Choice Specs is the admin's raw damage; Krookodile's Moxie converts a knockout into a sweep. Mega Golurk with Iron Fist is the ace and its Shadow Punch and Drain Punch are both boosted. Counterplay & audit: one large hit on Coalossal rather than two, weather replacement to strip Sand Rush, Wide Guard against the paired Rock lane, and Water/Ground pressure. Could this team be cooler? The previous build had Dragapult firing Surf at its own Coalossal - a 4x super effective Water attack on a Rock/Fire ally, which is a genuine design defect, not a clever combo, and Dragapult is a Ghost dragon with no Magma identity. It is cooler now because removing the broken self-hit turns Coalossal into the fight's real puzzle, because Excadrill here foreshadows the Mega Excadrill Tabitha brings to the Space Center, and because Volcanion - literally the volcano Pokemon - is the correct legendary for a man who digs into volcanoes.
crack: Kill Coalossal in one action or leave it alone entirely; replace the sand to halve Excadrill; Water, Ground and Fighting pressure; Mega Golurk is fast for its class but folds to Ghost, Dark, Grass and Water.

## E0338 TRAINER_COURTNEY_MAGMA_HIDEOUT class=admin
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Courtney is the faction's analyst and her battle is the sun engine executed properly. First two useful turns: Ninetales' Drought is automatic; its own action is Encore, which locks a player who took a setup or protect turn into repeating it, or a sun-boosted Heat Wave. Great Tusk's Protosynthesis triggers on that sun and raises its already enormous Attack, so Headlong Rush lands on turn one. Alternate deployment: Chi-Yu's Beads of Ruin cuts every other Pokemon's Special Defense by 25 percent for as long as it is on the field - a permanent, field-wide debuff that makes Ninetales' and Ceruledge's special lanes hit far harder than their stats suggest, and the player will not see it in the battle text. Reserves & disruption: Salazzle's Corrosion poisons Steel and Poison types, which is how Courtney answers the Steel walls the player brings to a Fire team; Ceruledge's Bitter Blade heals it for half the damage dealt and Weak Armor turns the player's physical answer into +2 Speed. Mega Pyroar is the ace and Competitive means any stat drop hands it +2 Special Attack. Counterplay & audit: remove Chi-Yu early even though it is not the immediate threat, replace the sun, and do not use Intimidate or Icy Wind into Competitive. Could this team be cooler? The previous build's sixth was a second Houndoom, which duplicated Flash Fire with the rest of the team and added nothing. It is cooler now with Ceruledge: a Fire/Ghost blade spirit gives Courtney a physical lane, Ghost coverage against the Psychic answers to her, self-healing that makes chip damage pointless, and Weak Armor so that the player's physical response accelerates her rather than her opponent. Chi-Yu's invisible team-wide Special Defense cut remains the fight's best hidden interaction.
crack: Kill Chi-Yu first; change the weather; never lower Pyroar's stats; Rock, Water and Ground pressure; Salazzle's Corrosion means a Steel wall is not a safe long-term answer.

## E0339 TRAINER_MAXIE_MAGMA_HIDEOUT class=leader
strategy: TRICK_ROOM,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Maxie wants to raise the continent, so the boss fight is the land itself refusing to move at your speed. First two useful turns: Stakataka sets Trick Room - it has zero Speed IVs and 13 base Speed, so it moves last in normal order and first under its own room, and Wide Guard is its alternative when the player's answer to six slow bodies is a spread move. Under the reversed order every other member outruns almost the entire player team. Alternate deployment: Mega Camerupt is the payoff. Quiet, zero Speed, Solid Rock, and Eruption at full HP is 150 base power into both slots from a Mega Special Attack stat - the single largest attack in the campaign to this point - and Trick Room means it fires first rather than last. Reserves & disruption: Regirock uses Curse, which is normally a liability because it lowers Speed; under Trick Room lowering Speed makes it faster, so Curse is pure profit and it becomes an unkillable +2 attacker. Copperajah's Heavy Metal and Rhyperior's Solid Rock are the two bodies that hold the field while the room ticks; Rhyperior's Lightning Rod also draws the Electric attacks aimed at nothing else here. Counterplay & audit: Taunt the setter, Protect or stall the five turns and win the turns after, Fake Out to deny the room turn, Haze the Curses, and chip Mega Camerupt so Eruption weakens. Could this team be cooler? The previous build was Torkoal/Crobat/Mightyena/Victreebel/Hydreigon/Mega Camerupt - which is very nearly the same team Maxie already brought to Mt Chimney, plus a Hydreigon that belongs to no faction. Fighting the same man twice with the same sun-and-Tailwind plan is the weakest thing a villain leader can do. It is cooler now because the second Maxie fight is a completely different machine from the first: Mt Chimney was sun and speed, the hideout is Trick Room and stone, and the same Mega Camerupt that was a slow liability there is the whole point here. Curse under Trick Room, Beast Boost on a fortress, and a full-HP Eruption arriving first are three interactions the player has not seen from this faction before.
crack: Taunt, Fake Out or a fast knockout on Stakataka denies the room; Protect and switch through the five turns; Haze undoes Curse; Water, Ground, Fighting and Grass pressure all bite hard, and chipping Camerupt directly reduces Eruption's power.

## E0340 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_1 class=grunt
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The Aqua Hideout is a flooded ship's hold and this is the bilge watch. First two useful turns: Wailord opens with Water Spout at full HP - 150 base power into both of the player's slots - so the crew's whole opening is built around Wailord acting before it is chipped, and Mantine's Wide Guard is there to keep the player's spread answer from resolving first. Tentacruel applies Icy Wind for speed control or Sludge Bomb. Alternate deployment: Dhelmise is the trap in the room; Steelworker gives its Steel attacks STAB it should not have, so Heavy Slam off a Ghost/Grass body punishes the Fairy and Ice answers the player brings to a Water team. Reserves & disruption: Mantine's Tailwind is the alternative to Wide Guard when the crew is behind on speed. Counterplay & audit: chip Wailord before it Spouts, Electric and Grass pressure, and remember Liquid Ooze-adjacent draining is not in play - this is a straight damage race. Could this team be cooler? The old block's third slot was Poipole, an Ultra Beast from another dimension standing in a pirate hideout, which is exactly the kind of "rare equals cool" substitution this audit is meant to remove. It is cooler now because every member is something you would actually find in a flooded hold - a whale, a jellyfish, an anchor and a manta - and because the room states the Aqua thesis in one move: act before Water Spout or lose both slots. Mantine's Wide Guard also teaches the player early that this faction protects its spread turns.
crack: Chip or Fake Out Wailord before it Spouts; Electric and Grass pressure; Dhelmise is slow and folds to Fire and Dark; spread moves are answered by Wide Guard, so aim single-target on the turn you expect it.

## E0341 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_2 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The boarding party. First two useful turns: Sharpedo's Speed Boost climbs every turn, so its first action can be Protect purely to bank a boost when the board is unsafe - this is one of the few places in the campaign where an empty-looking Protect is genuinely correct - and Crawdaunt's Adaptability Crabhammer is immediate damage that does not wait. Alternate deployment: Cramorant is the room's signature. Gulp Missile means Surf leaves it holding a fish, and when the player next attacks Cramorant the missile fires back for a quarter of the attacker's maximum HP and drops its Defense; the crew wants the player to answer Cramorant with a direct attack. Reserves & disruption: Dragalge's Adaptability Draco Meteor and Sludge Bomb are the special lane and it is immune to the Toxic answers a Poison-heavy hideout invites. Counterplay & audit: Fairy, Electric, Grass and Fighting pressure; do not attack a Cramorant that has already Surfed; Fake Out denies the Sharpedo boost turn. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Sharpedo/Dragalge/Overqwil/Crawdaunt, four Dark-and-Poison fish with the same job and no interaction between them. It is cooler now because Cramorant converts the room from a damage pile into a guessing game with a visible tell - the bird is holding a fish, and the player can see it - and because two Adaptability attackers of opposite categories force the player to split their defensive answer.
crack: Ignore a loaded Cramorant or remove it with a spread move; Fairy and Fighting answer the Dark pair; Sharpedo is frail and Fake Out or priority beats the boost clock.

## E0342 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_3 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The hull breakers, and the hideout's setup room. First two useful turns: Barbaracle takes Shell Smash behind Toxapex's Wide Guard or Seismitoad's immediate damage, and Tough Claws then makes a +2 Razor Shell and Stone Edge lethal in one turn. Drednaw's Shell Armor denies the critical hit that would otherwise end the setup plan, and it can take the second Shell Smash if the first is answered. Alternate deployment: if both Smash users are threatened, the room reverts to Seismitoad's Water Absorb attrition and Toxapex's Regenerator stall. Reserves & disruption: Toxapex's Baneful Bunker-free set is deliberate - it protects with Wide Guard for the side rather than itself, which is the harder and more useful action here. Counterplay & audit: Haze, Clear Smog, Fake Out, Taunt and priority all beat a two-turn setup plan; Electric and Grass pressure removes the Water bodies. Could this team be cooler? The old block put Scrafty - a Magma-side street thug, now correctly housed in the Magma Hideout - next to two shell Pokemon, so the room's idea was half-formed. It is cooler now because it is unambiguously the setup room: two Shell Smash bodies, one of which has Shell Armor specifically so the player cannot crit their way out, behind a Wide Guard that blanks the spread answer. The room asks a clean question - can you remove a 55-Speed sweeper in the one turn before it becomes a 110-Speed one?
crack: Haze or Clear Smog, Fake Out, Taunt, or a spread move on the Smash turn; Electric and Grass pressure; Wide Guard only covers spread moves, so single-target damage still lands.

## E0343 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_5 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The rain callers, and the first automatic weather in the hideout. First two useful turns: Politoed's Drizzle is free and Damp Rock holds it eight turns, so Politoed's own action is Helping Hand into Barraskewda's Swift Swim Liquidation or Icy Wind to bank a speed advantage for after the rain. Barraskewda at doubled Speed outruns essentially the entire player roster at this stage and its rain-boosted Water attacks are the room's damage. Alternate deployment: Kilowattrel's Wind Power charges from Tailwind and its Thunder never misses in rain, so the room can layer speed control on top of weather. Reserves & disruption: Jellicent absorbs the Water attacks the player brings against a rain team and Water Spout under rain is a second spread threat. Counterplay & audit: weather replacement is the single best answer, followed by Electric and Grass pressure and Taunt on the Tailwind. Could this team be cooler? It was already the strongest Aqua block and it survives largely intact - the concrete change is that Politoed is now the hideout's only Drizzle user, because Matt was also carrying one and a faction reads as orchestrated only when the engine has a single owner per room. Asked again after the revision: the room could add a fifth trick, but it should not; this is the plain, correct statement of the rain engine that Matt then escalates, and its value is that the player learns the pattern here and pays for it later.
crack: Replace the weather; Electric and Grass pressure; Taunt Politoed before Helping Hand; Barraskewda is frail and dies to priority or a spread move.

## E0344 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_7 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The ice hold, and a deliberate variation rather than a repeat of the rain room. First two useful turns: nothing here has Drizzle - Lapras sets Rain Dance by hand off a Damp Rock, which costs a real turn, and the room is built so that turn is worth paying for. Beartic's Swift Swim doubles off that manual rain and Cloyster's Skill Link Icicle Spear fires five hits every time, which is the answer to Focus Sash, Sturdy, Disguise and substitutes all at once. Alternate deployment: Cloyster can Shell Smash instead when the board is safe; its White Herb is deliberately absent so the defence drops are real and the player has a window. Reserves & disruption: Kingler's Sheer Force Crabhammer is boosted by both the rain and the ability, and Lapras' Freeze-Dry hits the Water answers the player brings for 2x. Counterplay & audit: Taunt or knock out Lapras before the manual rain, Fighting and Rock pressure on the Ice bodies, Steel typing against Icicle Spear. Could this team be cooler? The old block had a second Barraskewda - the exact species the previous room already used - plus Thievul, a Galarian fox with no maritime identity. It is cooler now because it answers "how do you do rain twice without repeating yourself": this room has to earn its weather, which makes Lapras a real target rather than scenery, and it pairs the weather with multi-hit and Freeze-Dry lanes that the automatic rain room does not have. Killing Lapras on turn one is the correct play and the room knows it.
crack: Remove or Taunt Lapras before Rain Dance and the room loses its engine; Fighting, Rock and Steel answer the Ice lane; Cloyster's Smash turn leaves it fragile.

## E0345 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 4
plan: The quartermaster's cabin, and the only grunt in either hideout who has been trusted with a Mega Stone. First two useful turns: Clawitzer's Mega Launcher doubles every pulse move it owns, and the room's signature is that this includes Heal Pulse - a full half of an ally's maximum HP restored in one action, from a grunt. The crew's plan is therefore to keep Mega Eelektross alive through damage the player expected to be lethal. Crobat Taunts the setup or support the player needs, or pivots with U-turn. Alternate deployment: Mega Eelektross has no weaknesses while Levitate holds and Wild Charge plus Crunch covers the field; it is the ace and the Mega slot. Reserves & disruption: Alolan Muk's Poison/Dark typing is immune to Psychic and its Knock Off strips the item the player was relying on. Counterplay & audit: Taunt or Encore the healer, burst damage that outpaces a 50 percent heal, Ground attacks the moment Levitate is off, and Fairy pressure on the Dark bodies. Could this team be cooler? The old block had the same four members but Clawitzer was a plain three-pulse attacker, which wasted the one ability in the game that turns a support move into a game-changer. It is cooler now because a grunt with a Mega and a doubled Heal Pulse is a genuine difficulty spike that the player will remember and complain about in the right way - and because the counterplay is clean and teachable: Taunt the shrimp, not the eel.
crack: Taunt, Encore or Knock Off the Clawitzer; burst the Eelektross past the heal rather than chipping it; Fairy and Ground pressure; Crobat folds to Rock, Electric and Psychic.

## E0346 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_6 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The pressure crew, guarding the pump room, and the most spiteful four Pokemon in the hideout. First two useful turns: Toxicroak's Fake Out buys the turn, and Pyukumuku is the reason the room is dangerous - Innards Out means that whoever lands the knockout blow immediately takes damage equal to Pyukumuku's remaining HP, so the player must choose between leaving a Recover-spamming wall alive and killing it into a full-HP retaliation. Wishiwashi opens in Solo Form and flips to School Form above a quarter HP, so it looks like free damage and is not. Alternate deployment: Overqwil's Intimidate plus Swords Dance is the physical lane once the stall bodies have soaked two turns. Reserves & disruption: Toxicroak's Dry Skin heals it on the Water attacks the player brought for this whole hideout. Counterplay & audit: chip Pyukumuku with a Pokemon you are willing to lose, or ignore it; Psychic and Ground pressure on the Poison pair; Electric and Grass on Wishiwashi. Could this team be cooler? The old block repeated Toxapex from the hull-breaker room and finished with Falinks, a Roman phalanx of bugs that has no place on a pirate crew. It is cooler now because it commits fully to being the attrition room - Innards Out, Schooling and Dry Skin are three separate ways of making the player's damage not count - and because Innards Out is the single most memorable "wait, what just happened" interaction available at this stage and no other trainer in the campaign has it.
crack: Knock out Pyukumuku with an expendable body or leave it entirely; Psychic, Ground and Electric pressure; Wishiwashi is trivial once below a quarter HP, so commit fully rather than chipping.

## E0347 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_8 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE

Golisopod's First Impression is priority Bug damage on its first turn out, so Emergency Exit switching it back in later reloads that move. Alternate deployment: Relicanth's Rock Head makes Head Smash recoil-free - 150 base power with no drawback, off a Pokemon nobody prepares for. The old block had Vileplume and Mismagius - a flower and a ghost witch standing in a pirate hideout for no stated reason. Relicanth's recoil-free Head Smash is the memorable piece - the player expects a harmless fossil and takes 150 base power.
crack: Haze or knock out Gyarados before the dance; Electric and Fairy pressure; Grass and Fighting on Relicanth and Walrein; First Impression only works on Golisopod's first turn out, so trade with it after that.

## E0348 TRAINER_MATT class=admin
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: 5
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Matt at the hideout is the same man the player fought on Mt Pyre, escalated rather than replaced - Pelipper is his bird both times, and the difference is what stands behind it. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle is free on entry and Wide Guard covers the crew's first spread turn while Kingdra, at doubled Swift Swim Speed and holding Choice Specs, fires a rain-boosted Hydro Pump before the player's lead moves. Alternate deployment: Grimmsnarl's Prankster gives priority Reflect and Light Screen, so the admin's screens land before the player's first attack and every subsequent exchange runs at half damage - this, not raw power, is what makes Matt harder than his grunts. Reserves & disruption: Archaludon's Electro Shot charges instantly in rain and fires a 130 base power Electric attack while gaining +1 Special Attack every time; it is the answer to the Water-resist walls the player brings. Basculegion's Last Respects grows 50 base power for every fainted teammate, so the longer the fight runs the more the admin's final slot hurts. Mega Malamar with Contrary turns Superpower's drops into +1/+1 every hit. Counterplay & audit: Brick Break, Defog or simply outlasting the screens; weather replacement; Taunt on Grimmsnarl before the screens resolve. Could this team be cooler? The previous build's Drizzle came from a Politoed that the grunt two rooms earlier already owned, which made the admin look like a bigger grunt instead of the same recurring villain. It is cooler now with Pelipper carried over from Mt Pyre: the player recognises the opening, expects the Mt Pyre plan, and gets screens, an instant Electro Shot and a Last Respects clock instead. Asked again after the revision - the team is a genuine boss because it wins by compounding advantages rather than by one big attack, and each of the four engines has a separate, honest answer.
crack: Taunt Grimmsnarl or break the screens; replace the rain to halve Kingdra and slow Electro Shot; kill Basculegion early while Last Respects is still weak; Fairy and Bug pressure on Malamar, Electric and Rock on Pelipper.

## E0493 TRAINER_SIDNEY class=elite
strategy: TAILWIND
mega_slots: 5,6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Dark Aura pressure with protected fast finishers. Replace Darkrai with Light Clay Grimmsnarl and Hoopa-Unbound with Speed Boost Sharpedo. Mega Sharpedo gets Strong Jaw for Crunch/Psychic Fangs, not Liquidation. Dark Aura boosts eligible Dark attacks on both sides. Could it be cooler? Sharpedo can earn a Speed Boost before Mega Evolution, then trade its ability for Strong Jaw; the Mega decision is a timing choice. Psychic Fangs removes screens. Mega Absol Z uses its151 base Speed and Magic Bounce to punish direct status targeting; it does not grant teamwide reflection. Overrepresented? Sharpedo is also an Archie showcase; Sidney adds Dark Aura and a second Dark Mega rather than another rain room. Absol Z stays Sidney's exclusive named form. Darkrai/Hoopa are released for more deliberate homes rather than included only for their power. Medium cap96 levels 100/96/100/97/98/96 are initial design seeds; complete battle calibration remains pending.
crack: Fairy pressure, burns on eligible physical attackers, screen removal, Trick Room, priority and denying Sharpedo a safe setup/activation window. Dark types obstruct Grimmsnarl Prankster Thunder Wave. No Dark Void coin flip is required.

## E0494 TRAINER_PHOEBE class=elite
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 4,6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Ghost trapping, Encore and boost punishment. Mega Banette replaces Dusclops. Mega Gengar keeps trapping/control; Banette supplies Poltergeist/Encore/Will-O-Wisp/Protect. Lunala replaces Calm Mind with Wide Guard and gets Timid/Cloak. Keep Giratina, Marshadow and Specs Spectrier. Stats: Physical Giratina supports and survives; Lunala and Spectrier attack specially; Marshadow/Banette exploit physical lanes. Banette uses its165 Mega Attack with HP investment and priority utility. Gengar receives95 as a starting seed for170 Special Attack/130 Speed; this is not proven balanced. Could it be cooler? Gengar can trap a foe while Banette Encores an already-used move. Lunala's Wide Guard covers applicable spread attacks; Marshadow steals opposing boosts. This is a coordinated trap/control team, without inventing Perish Song. Overrepresented? Gengar and Banette exist earlier as individual Megas; Phoebe combines them and has no species overlap with the other four League teams. Keep its ghost identity rather than add another weather setter.
